- **2026-11-09 — The finite-graph lemma of the C4 completion interface §3 now has a formal form.** `Psc.QuantifierInterface.inevMax_iff_of_finite`: with a finite reachable set, every maximal trajectory (an infinite path, or a finite one ending at a childless state) meets a target iff no state reached through non-targets lies on a cycle of non-targets or is a dead end of the full graph. This is item 2 of the prose lemma in [c4-ordered-word-completion-interface-2026-11-02.md](c4-ordered-word-completion-interface-2026-11-02.md) §3; item 1 is the definition of (E). `stub_inevitable_not_productive` records that the infinite-path reading (A) is vacuous at a dead end, which matters for a retained packet graph that drops split descendants. Graph logic only. Receipt (local): `ProofBankAudit.lean`, 4,249 declarations, 3,201 theorems, standard axioms.

  Locator: `proof/PscVerif/PscVerif/QuantifierInterface.lean` (`InevMax`, `DeadEnd`, `inevMax_iff`, `inevMax_iff_of_finite`); [audit, part I §1](quantifier-transfer-audit-2026-11-09.md).

- **2026-11-10 — Packet square: the three obligations are separate Lean hypotheses, and the literature gate is "proceed, narrowed".** `Psc.GraphRefinement` (graph logic only) states completeness, soundness from some source, soundness from every source, target preservation and target reflection separately. It proves the transfer of each reading under exactly the hypotheses it needs; for example, (AE) projects under `Complete` + `SoundEvery` + `TargetDown` and lifts under `Complete` + `SoundEvery` + `TargetUp`. It also proves that soundness from every source gives infinite path lifting with no branching hypothesis, that prefix lifts give it under finite branching (König), and that `PscVerif.QuotientDistance.Refinement` is the finite functional instance (`quotientDistance_iff`).
  - **Do not re-derive.** Each dropped hypothesis has a countermodel: `dropped`/`undrawn` (completeness), `overdrawn`/`overdrawnFork` (soundness), `broken` (soundness from some source only), `unroll` and `tail` (a quotient cycle need not be a cycle of sources, even over a finite actual graph), `fan` (prefix lifts without an infinite lift).
  - **Literature.** The square is textbook simulation theory (Katoen, lecture 24: simulation order and ∀CTL*/∃CTL*, stated for finite systems without terminal states, so nothing is imported). The two-track address graph is the prefix–suffix automaton (Canterini–Siegel 2001) taken in pairs, as in the boundary graph of Siegel–Thuswaldner 2009 (unit Pisot only) and the potential-overlap graph of Akiyama–Lee. Berthé–Steiner–Thuswaldner–Yassawi Theorem 3.1 gives full recognizability for aperiodic points when `rk(M_σ) = |A|`; it says nothing about finite balanced pairs.
  - **Mojo packet graph, read for the design pass (not yet repository evidence).** A reader of `kernel/psc/target_packets.mojo` reports that each packet edge is replayed against one literal inflation, but that the projection from packets to balanced-pair states has `Complete` and not `SoundEvery`. On `1/02/202`, 1,684 of 1,970 (expandable packet, child ordinal) pairs have no packet edge. Packet `success` is not the balanced-pair coincidence target: an ordinal-0 coincidence child is a non-success terminal. And the Python completeness assertion is a fixed-point check: a forged archive with root-unreachable mirrored packets passes it when given matching counts. These are leads for the design pass, not verified findings.

  Locator: [packet-square stop/go](packet-square-stop-go-2026-11-10.md); `proof/PscVerif/PscVerif/GraphRefinement.lean`.

- **2026-11-10 — The target-packet oracle now checks reachability from roots, not only closure.** `tests/test_target_packet_evidence.py` compared the dumped packet set with "roots plus the literal successors of every dumped packet". That is a fixed-point condition: a superset closed under the transition passed it. A reader of the packet-square pass built such a superset of the archived `real-secondary.jsonl`, adding 33 mirrored, root-unreachable packets and 69 edges, and the oracle accepted it when given matching counts. The archives themselves stayed protected by the pinned counts, the byte comparison and the SHA-256 manifests. `verify_dump` now also asserts that every packet is reachable from a root, so the dump is the least closed set. The new negative control `test_root_unreachable_closed_packets_are_refused` builds the same kind of forgery in memory and expects that assertion. A mutated copy without the assertion fails the control (it is then caught later, by the header's split-descendant count, which the in-memory forgery does not regenerate). Not yet checked for the same pattern: `tests/test_ordered_hub_evidence.py`.

  Locator: `tests/test_target_packet_evidence.py` (`verify_dump`, `test_root_unreachable_closed_packets_are_refused`).

- **2026-11-10 — Occurrence addresses and the residual transport law are now in `PscVerif`.** `Psc.OccurrenceAddress` has: `cutAddr` and `cutAddr_spec` (every position of `σ w` is digit `r` of the image of `w[i]`, with the prefix written out); `take_subst_digit` (the converse); `blockStart` and `getElem_red` (the `j`-th block of `red u v` is the aligned factor at its start offset, so a child is fixed by its parent and ordinal); `cut_defect_transport` and `cut_residual_zero` (the `R1Source` law, with its column hypothesis discharged by the definition of `inc`); and `residual_transport`. That last theorem says: measured from any balanced cut at or before both inflated positions, the prefix Parikh difference is `M · (u.take i − v.take j) + (digit defect)`. This is the packet update `r' = M r + λ` proved about literal prefixes. It needs no hypothesis that the two positions fall in the same child, so survival is a separate condition. The Tribonacci seed row calibrates it (`trib_child_residual`: residual `(0, 1, −1) = M(e₀ − e₁)`). The address and transport lemmas were first scratch-checked by a reader of the packet-square pass. Previously the law existed only in the standalone `proof/psc_routes/` modules and in prose.
- **2026-11-10 — Correction to the oracle entry above:** `tests/test_ordered_hub_evidence.py` does not reconstruct the packet set; it reads the target-aware dumps that `tests/test_target_packet_evidence.py` verifies, so the fixed-point gap does not recur there.

  Locator: `proof/PscVerif/PscVerif/OccurrenceAddress.lean`.

- **2026-11-10 — Quantifier audit, part II: the source-bound crosswalk.** Sections 10-20 of [the audit note](quantifier-transfer-audit-2026-11-09.md) cover:
  - the reading ((E), (AE), (AE°), (A); one seed, every seed or every vertex) of each load-bearing statement;
  - a transfer matrix over the C4, G1, direct-route, mass and R1 bridges;
  - the stage-by-stage type-check of mass loss → physical overlap → literal Sync;
  - the finite-domain, family and capped results with the scope of their evidence;
  - the status surfaces that can be misread;
  - the PR crosswalk;
  - 45 findings and 20 work items.

  How it was produced: six read-only route maps at `c5ed4e5`, one synthesis and one adversarial verifier per hazard class, then a writer and a critic. The critic re-opened about 250 locators and found 4 wrong; all were fixed. All 111 route-map hazards are mapped to rows (§20). Nothing was executed by the readers.

- **2026-11-10 — Packet square, wave A (Lean): the literal occurrence graph and the embedding layer.** Two new modules.
  - `Psc.OccurrenceGraph` (65 theorems) defines a vertex `Occ` as an ordered block, one selected position per track, and a birth record (oriented parent, ordinal). On it:
    - `occStep` and `occStar` are the literal one-step descendants, with splits recorded by their two ordinals;
    - the addendum's fields are definitions (`topAddr`/`botAddr`, digits, `Valid`, `swap`, `residual`, `Interior`/`Boundary`, `Coincident`/`SuccessFor`);
    - `residual_step` derives `r' = M r + λ` along every literal edge from `residual_transport`, and `residual_iterate_lt` carries it along a finite path;
    - `occGraph` and `occGraphFor` are the two targets;
    - controls: Fibonacci `fibDead` (a valid occurrence pair whose only digit pair splits, over a state with a balanced-pair child), and the archived rows 209 and 429 of `1/02/202` decoded as a literal 2-cycle of `occGraph`, with the archived addresses, residuals `(-1,1,0)` and `(1,-1,1)` and the transport law re-derived by `decide`.
  - `Psc.GraphEmbedding` (42 theorems) has `Refines` and `Embeds`, the (A⁺) and dead-end transfers, finite path lifting (`prefixLift_finite`, the analogue of `QuotientDistance.path_lift` on arbitrary types), `exists_lift_of_finite_sources` with both hypotheses shown necessary (`fan_finite_sources_no_lift`, `chains_finite_sources_no_lift`), and `Embeds.cyclicProductive_iff`.
  - **Do not re-derive.** The design pass's critic found that `Table.check_sound` is false over a merely ordered alphabet: with an empty `<` an accepted table can list a state and its swap, and two rows decode to one occurrence pair. Its injectivity needs `[LinearOrder α]`. `broken` separates `SoundSome` from `SoundEvery` only along finite paths (its quotient has no infinite path); the infinite case is `brokenLoop_no_lift`.
  - Each module had an adversarial review; no blocker or major finding, and the minor docstring findings were fixed. Graph logic and literal rows only: nothing here says that any packet table is sound or complete.

  Locator: `proof/PscVerif/PscVerif/{OccurrenceGraph,GraphEmbedding}.lean`.

- **2026-11-10 — Packet square, wave B (Lean): the absolute layer and the reading ρ.** `Psc.OccurrenceRealisation` (95 theorems) defines absolute occurrence pairs in the iterates `σⁿ(T₀)` (`AbsOcc`, `absStep`), their block-local readings (`Anchor`, `Anchored`, `anchoredGraph`, `reading`), and the declared occurrence universe `Universe s T₀`: the set reached by retained edges from the level-0 root readings.
  - **What is proved about ρ.**
    - ρ is complete and sound from **every** anchored source, at every level and offset (`reading_soundEvery`).
    - Infinite path lifting along ρ is choice-free (`absLineage_anchor`).
    - The reading is a function on the universe (`universe_reading_unique`), and its birth record is the actual retained parent (`universe_birth`).
    - The refinement is restated on the universe itself for both targets (`universe_refines`, `universe_refines_for`, `universe_readings`).
    - Completeness relative to the declared universe: every absolute pair inside a **noncoincident** irreducible block of `red(σⁿT₀)` is in the universe (`anchored_cover_universe`).
    - The block projection β is complete and not sound from every source (`fib_block_readings`).
  - **Correction to the design brief (scratch).** Its prose "the absolute pairs are those whose two positions lie in one irreducible block of `red(σⁿT₀.1, σⁿT₀.2)`" is false at coincidence blocks: `fib_lvl2_not_universe` and `anchored_cover_needs_hnc` (Fibonacci). The noncoincidence hypothesis is necessary.
  - **Do not re-derive.** A quotient packet cycle is realised only as an infinite absolute lineage graded by level, never as an absolute cycle. The Tribonacci positive control for ρ needs packet data and belongs to the data and controls work packages.
  - Adversarial review: no blocker or major finding; all nine minor findings were applied.

  Locator: `proof/PscVerif/PscVerif/OccurrenceRealisation.lean`.

- **2026-11-10 — Packet square, wave B (Lean): the packet certificate.** `Psc.PacketCertificate` (91 top-level theorems) defines:
  - the v1 record of an exported packet table (`FactorRow`, `PacketRow`, `StarRow`, `HubRow`, `Table`), and `Table.decode` / `Table.occAt` into literal occurrence pairs;
  - an eleven-clause `Bool` checker, `Table.check`, evaluated by one `decide +kernel` per table.

  On an accepted table:
  - `check_sound` (over a linearly ordered alphabet): every row decodes to a valid literal occurrence pair, and `occAt` is injective; every star is the literal `occStar`, splits included; addresses, letters, residual and bits are literal; every row is reachable from a root row.
  - `iso`: an isomorphism onto the induced subgraph of `occGraph` on a child-closed set, for both targets.
  - `range_eq_reach`: with no order hypothesis, the table is exactly the set reachable in `occGraph` from the root pairs of its noncoincident states.
  - `seeds_listed`, `bpaStatesNC_listed`; `roots`, `hubs_sound` and `hubs_rows`. Hub rows are checked for state range, noncoincidence and uniqueness, stricter than the plan.
  - Controls, each refused by `decide +kernel`, several at a single clause: `weird` shows that injectivity fails without a linear order; a duplicate key; a forged root-unreachable row, refused by the birth clause; out-of-range hub rows; and `fibExtra_birth_not_first`, which shows the checker accepts any earlier parent as birth, not only the first edge the exporter emits.
  - **Not checked:** that the listed states are exactly the normalised `bpaStatesNC`; `fibExtra` is accepted and is a counterexample to that converse.

  The four exporter data modules (Tribonacci, `1/02/202`, `1/012/1`, `1/2/021`) are accepted against this module in local runs. They are committed in the next wave. Adversarial review: no blocker or major finding; all minor findings were applied.

  Locator: `proof/PscVerif/PscVerif/PacketCertificate.lean`.

Other results: `covered_closed`, `covered_level_zero`, `covered_acyclic`, and `universe_covered` (the universe of a listed seed is covered). The new bridge `locate_eq_cutAddr` proves `locate (w.map s) p = some (cutAddr s w p)` for every `p < |σ w|`, so mainline `locateSubst` and the branch `cutAddr` name the same address; do not add a third. - **2026-11-15 — Minus Class A has a non-simple Parry Perron number, and its β-substitution has the same characteristic polynomial. Two scratch subdivision searches found no conjugacy at `(k,t)=(0,0)`.** With `n=k+t+3`, `d=k+2t+5`, `A=n+d` and `M=[[2,1,1],[0,1,1],[A,n,n−1]]`, the characteristic polynomial is `χ(λ)=λ³−(n+2)λ²−(t+3)λ+2`. Since `χ(n+2)=2−(t+3)(n+2)<0<χ(n+3)`, `β∈(n+2,n+3)`. The greedy expansion gives `T(1)=β−n−2`, digit `t+2`, remainder `1−2/β`, then digit `n`, remainder `β−n−2=T(1)`. Hence `d_β(1)=(n+2)((t+2)n)^ω`: a non-simple Parry number. Its β-substitution `τ: 1↦1^{n+2}2, 2↦1^{t+2}3, 3↦1^n 2` has trace `n+2`, determinant `−2` and characteristic polynomial `χ`; this was checked symbolically for `0≤k,t≤3`, and the β-expansion prefix was checked numerically. The 2026-10-07 entry tested simple-Parry polynomials only, so it does not exclude this family from Barge (2018). `σ` itself is not in β-substitution form: with `y` as the run letter it fails Barge's Property 2, since `xx` and `xc` are both legal. Orientation: `σ`'s inflation-fixed boundary pairs `x.x`, `x.c` share their left letter, `τ`'s `2.1`, `3.1` share their right letter, and the reversed `τᴿ` has `1.2`, `1.3`; this argument is not checked against a source. At scale `β` the lengths satisfy `|y|=β=|τ(1)|`, `|c|=β+1` and `|x|=|τ(1)|+|τ(2)|+|τ(3)|+1`. A letter-to-word subdivision `ψ` therefore has forced Parikh vectors `(n+2,1,0)`, `(n+3,1,0)` and `(2n+t+5,2,1)`. An exhaustive scratch search at `(0,0)` covered all 45,864 such `ψ`. For each it asked whether `ψσ=u⁻¹(τψ)u` or `τψ=u⁻¹(ψσ)u` for one word `u`. It found 0 hits against `τ` and 0 against `τᴿ`. A second scratch probe tested collar consistency (radius ≤6, shifts in `ℤ[β]` with coefficients ≤40 in absolute value and `|shift|≤3β`) and found no mutually-locally-derivable alignment in any of the four orientations. Status: exploration; the scripts are scratch and not committed. Neither search excludes a conjugacy at other scales, a non-subdivision local derivation, or a conjugacy that does not commute with inflation. Barge (2018)'s treatment of the non-unit case was not re-read this session. A positive identification would give pure discrete spectrum for the whole minus-Class-A family; it would not touch the universal premises.
  Locator: [class-a-uniform-zero-return-2026-11-02.md](class-a-uniform-zero-return-2026-11-02.md) (the family and `M`); [p1b-catch-up-free-ppvc-2026-10-07.md](p1b-catch-up-free-ppvc-2026-10-07.md) §3 (the simple-Parry scan); [p1b-barge-diamond-configuration-gate-2026-10-02.md](p1b-barge-diamond-configuration-gate-2026-10-02.md) (Barge's Properties 1–3).

- **2026-11-15 — Theorem CA: boundary hitting from the nine swap seeds holds on `𝒯_CA = {t ≤ 5} ∪ {t ≤ k−1}` of the minus class-A chamber; Corollary CA-1 gives #452's entry into Z and H there.** The class-B cone cover, made generic in Theorem E's class, certifies twelve families: the lines `t = 0..5`, the sector `t ≥ 6, k−t ≥ 6` (Theorem C's `D−` generators) and the lines `k−t = 1..5`. It finds 16 symbolic regions (largest 325 vertices) and 116 exact verdicts over 86 distinct members, all hitting and none non-PIP. The cone engine re-proves Theorem L″'s class-A rays from `k ≥ 8` (t=0) and `k ≥ 12` (t=1), against the Sturm engine's 104 and 124, with the same counts 325 and 303. The Transfer Lemma (S9 → S18 by reversal; seed-patch graph ≅ `Reach_Γ(S9)` as an identity of relations; the forward half of T0 re-proved) turns HIT9 into first-hit entry into Z and H for every vertex of Γ, transient ones included. Status: repository-proved, computer-assisted, human review pending; not registered in the claim ledger. The remainder `{t ≥ 6, t ≥ k}` (D ≥ 2) is open.
  Locator: [class-a-chamber-entry-stopgo-2026-11-15.md](class-a-chamber-entry-stopgo-2026-11-15.md) §4, §6; `evidence/class-a-chamber-cone-2026-11-15/`.

- **2026-11-15 — Tooling pitfall: the cone engine's sign-at-β rules cannot decide a quadratic whose leading coefficient changes sign on the region, and diagonal shifts do not help.** On the class-A sector cone, every shift 0–4 stopped on such a quadratic, with leading coefficient `s1 − (shift+2)`, so the zero moves with the shift. The sign itself is constant: the committed instance, the regression's element at `s ≥ (2,2)`, has `g(β) ≈ −1,659` at `s = (2,2)`. The fix is an opt-in Bernstein rule, `Cone.hull`: certify the middle Bernstein coefficient `b1 = G(L) + (U−L)G′(L)/2` together with the end values (Lemma S″). It is on for class A only, so Theorem C's class-B run and the class-C runs replay with their original decisions. A sound rule must still refuse a dip around β with positive ends; the regression checks this through the engine at `(k,t) = (8,0)`. Separately, `certify_cone` never certified the bracket order `L < U`, on which every sign rule already relied: a proposal `U = 0` passes `χ(U) > 0` because `χ(0) = 2`. The order is now certified, and a disorder refuses. On the final sources the class-A run is unchanged line for line, Theorem C's class-B plus and minus runs replay the archive exactly (regions, shifts, exact members, totals), and the cone, line and five class-C tests pass.
  Locator: `kernel/psc/symbolic_cone.mojo` (`bernstein_middle`); `kernel/tests/test_class_a_chamber_entry.mojo`.

- **2026-11-15 — The minus class-A chamber is the class-A twin of class B's minus lines `(3q−g, q, q−1)`, and Theorem L″'s rays `A 2 2 1 0 1 -1` and `A 2 3 1 0 1 -1` are the chamber rays t = 0 and t = 1.** With `K = k+1`, `A = 3n−K`. The twins share `M`, χ, the left Perron vector, strictness and S9; they differ only in the endings of `σ(y)`, so their children and seed graphs differ. Machinery transfers (brackets, middle sign geometry `h = 1/β`, `v = Kh`); certificates do not, in either direction. Class B's `WINDOW = 6`, strict-core box, anchored band and potential tables do not transfer.
  Locator: [class-a-chamber-entry-stopgo-2026-11-15.md](class-a-chamber-entry-stopgo-2026-11-15.md) §3, §4 (Lemma 0).

R-β (Barge 2018 by conjugacy or MLD) is DEFERRED: the critical-exponent obstruction sketch is unverified, and three checks are owed. R-B (bounded backward depth from H) and R-L (per-label stars) are redirected on [F] only. The `u ≤ 1/5` sign box stays non-transferable (2026-10-26 entry). The next target is the class-B-twin partition certificate on `{t ≥ 6, t ≥ k}`, with the strict-core closure as the default carrier.

- **2026-11-16 — on the geometric property (F) class bounded gaps are automatic, so there C4 ⟺ SC_all ⟺ pure discrete spectrum; the bounded-gap slack is empty on 1,794 of 4,554 specimens.** (F) means no periodic state with `v ≠ 0` of the prototile map `f(j,v) = (j′, Mv − p(P))`; it is Minervino–Thuswaldner's geometric property (F) and the no-terminal-leftmost-cycle class of Corollary LC5. Theorem FC1 (elementary written proof, not machine-checked): for `σ ∈ PIP3` with (F), every vertex of either of two fixed rays becomes a common vertex after a bounded number of inflations, so a strict system exists iff some pair of distinct `g`-periodic letters is not left strongly coincident. Theorem FC2 (conditional on Baker–Barge–Kwapisz 2006 and Barge–Diamond 2002): `cr = ν_L = ν_R ≤ 2` there, and the link to pure discrete spectrum is Baker–Barge–Kwapisz Cor. 5.2. Hence a proof of universal C4 would contain pure discrete spectrum for every ternary (F) substitution (Corollary H): on (F) the C4 route is not weaker than #138, which sharpens §4 of the finite-domain note ("the only content of C4 beyond #138 is the bounded-gap slack"). Pinned: (F) on 1,794 of 4,554 specimens, invariant under image reversal; 1,956 pairs of periodic first letters with 97,992 relative states, none absorbed; all 2,790 pairs on the other 2,760 specimens have a relative state absorbed by an interior cycle. Locators: [(F)-class note](c4-geometric-f-class-2026-11-16.md); [closure-attempt note §8](c4-closure-attempt-2026-11-16.md); `kernel/tests/test_c4_geometric_f.mojo`.

- **2026-11-16 — Theorem P: for odd determinant, coincidence rank 2 forces `tr M ≡ e₂(M) (mod 2)` as well as `dim H¹ ≥ 5`.** For `σ ∈ PIP3` with `det M` odd and `cr(σ) = 2`: `tr M ≡ e₂(M)` (`e₂` the sum of the principal 2×2 minors) and `dim H¹(Ω_σ;ℚ) ≥ 5`. Conditional on the imports listed with Theorem P in the (F)-class note: Barge 2015 as printed (Thm 4(5), Thm 5, Thm 12, Lemmas 13–14, Prop. 19, Lemma 20, the exact sequence (4.1), the count of periodic words in the proof of Thm 22, and the proper version of its §4), Barge–Diamond 2008 (Lemma 2, Corollary 3, Theorem 4), and the identification of the two coincidence ranks, derived in that note from Baker–Barge–Kwapisz Thm 4.2 and Thm 5.1 with a standard fact that was not read. The mechanism: every nonzero eigenvalue of the signed stack-overlap matrix `X − Y` is a root of unity, which fails modulo 2 exactly when `χ_M` is an irreducible cubic over the field with two elements. Re-derived by three verifiers; not machine-checked. What it excludes: `cr = 2` for odd determinant with `tr M ≢ e₂(M)`, beyond the `dim H¹ ≤ 4` case of Barge 2015 Thm 22. What it does not: `cr ≥ 3`; anything for even determinant; a strict system by itself (it needs `cr ≤ 2`, available on (F), giving Theorem FC3). The parity class is that of the degree-2 parity sieve (`kernel/psc/degree2_sieve.mojo`), which had given only a state-count floor. Pinned class counts: 2,628 odd-determinant corpus specimens, 2,136 with `dim H¹ ≤ 4`, 1,344 with `tr M ≢ e₂(M)`, 2,352 with either; of the 510 (F) specimens with two periodic letters on both sides, 156 are reached (84 by `dim H¹ ≤ 4`, 120 by parity) and 354 are not (174 with `det M = 2`, 180 odd). The finer refinement in the exponent `n` is scratch only. Locators: [(F)-class note](c4-geometric-f-class-2026-11-16.md); `kernel/tests/test_c4_geometric_f.mojo`.

- **2026-11-16 — the Barge–Diamond mechanism cannot deliver a cut pair, and its bounded-gap extension is false for reducible Pisot-inflation substitutions.** The maximal-configuration argument of Barge–Diamond 2002 runs in the length lattice for any nonerasing substitution whose inflation is a Pisot number of degree at least 2 (Theorem GBD: proof outline following Barge–Diamond's proof, re-derived by one landing verifier, not machine-checked, literature novelty not assessed; irreducibility and primitivity are not used; the conclusion is geometric, not equality of Parikh prefixes). Each branching exchanges a strongly coincident pair (a letter carried at a vertex against one not carried); a cut pair of a strict system is never strongly coincident and both its letters are carried at a common vertex; on three letters the good pair is always available, so a hub pair is never produced. The statement "(BG) two periodic rays with relatively dense common vertices share a tile" is false in that category: `1/012/101`, `1/021/202`, `10/102/211`, `01/201/112`, `1/012/121` and the new C-D1, C-D2 have tile-disjoint ray pairs with relatively dense common vertices. The balanced-pair strict systems of `1/012/101`, C-D1 and C-D2 are pinned; the geometric overlap closures and the census (126 of the 5,646 primitive reducible ternary substitutions with images of length at most 3 and irrational Pisot inflation have exactly one eventually coincident letter pair) are exploratory scratch observations confirmed by an independent scratch verifier. A closing argument must fail on these controls. Locators: [closure-attempt note §6.1, §7](c4-closure-attempt-2026-11-16.md); `kernel/tests/test_c4_reducible_pisot_controls.mojo`.

- **2026-11-16 — nonunit residue route: the seam criterion excluded no non-trivial nonunit corpus member (scratch; all have `det M = 2`); the families `F1_c`, `F3_c` are instances of the on-main eliminator.** Staggered-seam descent (Theorem T1) and the seam criterion (Proposition C2) restate the uniform zero-candidate eliminator of the bilateral-birth note §9 with the legal-predecessor refinement of the legal-seams and seam-birth notes; the one added lemma (Lemma RL) is that the words of a strict system rooted at periodic rays are legal, with no hub, track or phase chosen. `F1_c = (0^{c−1}1, 120^{c−1}, 0^c)` has empty on-main `L_σ` verbatim; `F3_c = (0^{c−1}10, 021, 20)` has exactly the on-main types `(1,2,2,0)`, `(2,0,1,2)`, removed because the bigram `12` is illegal. Both are PIP3 with `det M = c` and have no strict system for every `c ≥ 3` (hand tables in the note and the written proof of Proposition C2; PIP3 membership, `det M = c` and the empty legal birth-type list are pinned for `3 ≤ c ≤ 63`), and both keep legal types at `c = 2` (pinned). For `3 ≤ c ≤ 8` all letter pairs of every member are also left strongly coincident (levels `(4,1,5)`, `(1,4,2)`), so nothing beyond Theorem A(4) is excluded there. All 1,926 nonunit corpus members have `det M = 2`. Exploratory scratch observations: the criterion excludes none of the 606 of them that an endpoint collapse does not already exclude, and 54 reducible `det M = −2` substitutions with strict systems (found within caps) satisfy every residue condition. Route remark, not proved: on the tested sets the residue conditions of this seam criterion did not distinguish irreducible from reducible sources, so a uniform nonunit exclusion should not be re-attempted with this criterion alone. The route's wording "residues cannot see irreducibility" is not adopted (unproved, and false read literally), and nothing here bears on the matrix-adic carrier route on main. Locators: [closure-attempt note §5](c4-closure-attempt-2026-11-16.md); `kernel/psc/c4_seam_families.mojo`; `kernel/tests/test_c4_reducible_pisot_controls.mojo`.

- **2026-11-16 — the unique-partner premise of retired route 5 cannot hold for unit `dim H¹ = 3` (fork theorem).** For unit `σ ∈ PIP3` with `dim H¹(Ω_σ;ℚ) = 3` and a strict component `Z`, every `(T,T′) ∈ Z` has a second partner `T″` with `(T,T″) ∈ Z` and `Φⁿ(T′)`, `Φⁿ(T″)` disjoint for all `n`. So the top-track map is never injective, the two cut sets are disjoint, and an interior periodic address exists ((F) fails). Derived-substitution rigidity (cut-attractor note §7, "needs a unique partner everywhere") is therefore unavailable on that class, not merely unproved. Recorded proof conditional on the imports listed in the (F)-class note (Barge 2015, Baker–Barge–Kwapisz 2006, Barge–Diamond 2002 and 2008, and standard topological facts not read in a source); reconstructed by three verifiers from a sketch; not machine-checked; vacuous on every computed domain. Only non-injectivity is shown: whether the map is a covering is open, and nothing is known for `dim H¹ ≥ 4` or for nonunit `σ`. Locators: [(F)-class note, Theorem Fork](c4-geometric-f-class-2026-11-16.md); [closure-attempt note §3, §10](c4-closure-attempt-2026-11-16.md).

- **2026-11-16 — two new reducible strict controls with irrational Pisot inflation (C-D1, C-D2), pinned natively with the standing control `1/012/101`.** C-D1 `= 011/120/2012` (`det M = −1`, `χ = (x−1)(x²−3x−1)`, first-letter map the identity): `(X₀,X₁)` and `(X₀,X₂)` carry one closed system of 18 ordered states (longest 13, ordered first pairs `(0,1),(0,2)`, no state the swap of another) and `(X₁,X₂)` coincides (left witness at level 2, zero-based letter index 3). C-D2 `= 010/1222/2012` (`det M = 1`, `χ = (x−1)(x²−4x+1)`): 21 states for `(X₀,X₁)` and 23 for `(X₁,X₂)` (longest 56; the second contains the swap of every state of the first), and `(X₀,X₂)` coincides (level 3, letter index 14). `1/012/101` (`det M = 1`, `χ = (x+1)(x²−2x−1)`): 3 states under `σ²` from the transposed root `(X₀,X₁)`, 6 under `σ` (it is already one of the 28 pinned carriers of `kernel/tests/test_c4_quotient_spectrum.mojo`: 6 states under `σ`, rank 2, quotient root `−1`; new here are its 3-state `σ²` system, the factorisation of `χ_M` and the screen behaviour). Each is primitive with an irreducible Pisot quadratic cofactor, so only irreducibility of `χ_M` is missing. C-D1 and C-D2 realise the full three-ray hub configuration (hub strictly paired with bounded gaps, good pair coincident) and refute any letter-, window- or seam-level C4 argument that does not use irreducibility; use them alongside `10/102/211`, which remains the nonunit control for residue arguments. Locators: `kernel/tests/test_c4_reducible_pisot_controls.mojo`; [closure-attempt note §7](c4-closure-attempt-2026-11-16.md).

- **2026-11-16 — tooling pitfalls from the C4 closure attempt.** (i) Overlap shifts must be enumerated in the lattice `ℓ·ℤ³`, not in `ℤ[λ]`: for `1/02/002` and `01/20/200` the index is 2 and the unrestricted enumeration gives spurious non-coincident overlaps (single-pass scratch finding). (ii) A scratch float-margin box enumeration is not evidence, and a ray prefix is not the set of relative states; the canonical decision of (F) is `psc.c4_geometric_f` (certified radii, exact arithmetic, raises on a cap). (iii) `psc.pisot.is_pisot_charpoly` accepts a cubic with the root `+1` (C-D1, C-D2), so outside `is_pip` it is not a Pisot filter by itself (pinned). (iv) On reducible controls "strict" has two senses: geometric common vertices need not be equal-Parikh cuts (about half are not for `1/012/101`, `1/021/202` and C-D2; scratch). (v) `left_witness` reports a zero-based letter index, not a cut ordinal. (vi) The on-main hub-based birth-seam lists are empty for `F1_2` as well (build-stage observation, not pinned), so they are no non-vacuity control for `F1`; `F3_2` has four nonempty choices (pinned). (vii) Baker–Barge–Kwapisz's "`Σ = Γ` for irreducible Pisot" defers to Barge–Kwapisz 2006, which was not obtained during the proof attempts (what was read of it at the audit is in the (F)-class literature note): cite Theorem R of the periodic-pair fibre gate; and `cr ≥ ν_R` is not literally their Remark 4.3. (viii) The provers' report files were refused by the harness, so route proofs existed only as structured summaries until written into the notes. (ix) `unresolved_local_birth_types` checks the determinant, not primitivity: the non-primitive `1/0/22` has an empty `L_σ`, `det M = −2` and the strict system `{(01,10),(10,01)}` (pinned); `legal_local_birth_types` refuses non-primitive input. (x) An empty search for interior cycles in an uncertified box is not (F): radius 0 finds nothing for `01/12/021`, which fails (F) (pinned); `interior_cycles_in_box` returns no verdict, and the certified radii are pinned because the cycle pins alone do not see a bound that is slightly too small. Locators: [closure-attempt note §2, §7, §11](c4-closure-attempt-2026-11-16.md); `kernel/tests/test_c4_geometric_f.mojo`; `kernel/tests/test_c4_reducible_pisot_controls.mojo`.

- **2026-11-16 — withdrawn or corrected figures and wordings of the C4 closure attempt.** (a) FC1 "maximal transient 38" is withdrawn: it came from 3,000-letter ray prefixes; over the complete relative-state closure the maximum is 51 `f`-steps, attained by 24 pairs (pinned; for `1/012/100`, pair `(0,1)`, a 3,000-letter prefix shows 60 of 240 relative states and transient 36). (b) "90 such class-D substitutions" after "unimodular, Pisot inflation": only 36 of the 90 have irrational Pisot inflation, 54 are singular (scratch). (c) "a level-4 left witness for each family member": the levels are `(4,1,5)` for `F1_c` and `(1,4,2)` for `F3_c` (pinned for `c ≤ 8`). (d) "`X_a` and `X_b` coincide on a dense open set": wrong; the correct statement is eventual coincidence on an open dense set with shared tiles of density 1. (e) "phase one cannot occur at coincidence rank 2": flat phase one only. (f) The partner-orientation lemma "for any primitive `τ`": false, `012/120/201`. (g) "the top-track map is not a covering map": only non-injectivity is shown, for unit `dim H¹ = 3`. (h) "`ℤ/2` for `1/012/1`": that control is periodic with tile/return quotient `ℤ/3`. Locators: [closure-attempt note §11](c4-closure-attempt-2026-11-16.md); `kernel/tests/test_c4_geometric_f.mojo` (`test_a_ray_prefix_is_not_the_set_of_relative_states`).

- **2026-11-16 — flat ordered strict systems force coincidence rank at least 3; under (F) every ordered sink of a strict system is swap-symmetric (no strict system is flat).** For `σ ∈ PIP3`, a finite child-closed set `B` of ordered irreducible noncoincident balanced pairs of legal words with `B ∩ swap(B) = ∅` gives three pairwise never-coincident strands over one point, so `cr ≥ 3` (imports: the definition of `cr` in Baker–Barge–Kwapisz 2006 and return module `= ℤ³`, Theorem R on main). Contrapositive: at `cr ≤ 2` every ordered sink of a strict system is swap-symmetric; with Theorem FC2, a strict system of an (F) substitution has only swap-symmetric sinks. A flat phase-one system also forces an interior `f`-periodic state. Confirmed with corrections at landing; hand-verified, not machine-checked, and vacuous on every computed domain (every strict system found has reducible `χ_M`). It does not exclude phase one: a transposed root with a mixed system is untouched, and the remaining statement (N) is implied by strong coincidence of the transposed pair. Locator: [closure-attempt note §6.2, §10](c4-closure-attempt-2026-11-16.md).

- **2026-11-16 — round 2 of the C4 closure attempt: three routes, universal C4 still open; the open class on (F) shrinks, one more route is retired.** Round 2 attacked the open statements of §10 of the closure-attempt note (the `cr = 2` wall first), the recorded non-claim on infinitude of the FC3 class, and the formal debt on Theorem FC1. Route cr2-wall: partial (Theorems FP, TT, NI, W, P+, H3 with Lemmas U, S+ and Corollaries FC3+, N+, and an obstruction); two independent verifiers, no refutation, corrections binding (attributions, the wording of the obstruction, "Theorem Fork for nonunit `σ`" downgraded to expected). Route fc3-family: partial (two infinite families); both verifiers confirmed every theorem, and one clause of its prior-art comparison was refuted and dropped. Route lean-fc1: Theorem FC1 machine-checked; the audit found no gap inside the machine-checked statements. C4, #138, #139, #84, G1 and PSC stay open and no claim status changes. On the (F) class "`ν_L = 2` is impossible" is now recorded as proved, conditionally, also for `dim H¹ = 3` at every determinant; it stays open for even determinant with `dim H¹ ≥ 4` and for odd determinant with `tr M ≡ e₂(M)` and `dim H¹ ≥ 5`. Pinned class counts (not open cases; the corpus has no strict system): of the 510 corpus (F) specimens with two periodic letters on both sides, 198 meet the hypotheses of FC3 or FC3+ and 312 = 180 odd + 132 with `det M = 2` do not. A new attempt should start from §15.3 of the closure-attempt note and say in advance which step fails on C-D1, C-D2 and `1/012/101`. Locators: [closure-attempt note §15](c4-closure-attempt-2026-11-16.md); [rank-two note](c4-rank-two-wall-2026-11-16.md); [family note](c4-fc3-family-2026-11-16.md); `kernel/tests/test_c4_geometric_f.mojo`.

- **2026-11-16 — Theorem H3: `dim H¹ = 3` excludes coincidence rank 2 for every determinant; with (F) it gives `cr = 1` (Corollary FC3+). New only for even determinant.** For `σ ∈ PIP3` with `dim H¹(Ω_σ;ℚ) = 3`, `cr(σ) ≠ 2`. For odd determinant this is Barge 2015 Thm 22 (do not cite its Cor. 25 for the `l = 0` statement: the `dim H¹` census note forbids it); the even-determinant case is what round 2 adds. Mechanism: every integral class of `H¹` is the class of an eigenfunction, unit or not ("Lemma 9′"; not new: Barge–Kellendonk–Schmieding 2012, arXiv:1204.1432, Cor. 3 and Thm 5, identified by the prior-art verifier; the prover's own derivation from Barge–Diamond 2008 Thm 4 and Baker–Barge–Kwapisz (5.1)–(5.3), Thm 5.1 is kept as the proof), then Barge's twin involution at rank 2 and Lemma 10 of the (F)-class note at `p = 2`. Corollaries: FC3+ ((F) and `dim H¹ = 3` give `cr = 1`: pure discrete spectrum, all pairs strongly coincident on both sides, no strict system) and N+ (a strict system with `dim H¹ = 3` forces `cr ≥ 3` and failure of (F)), both for every determinant. What it does not close: `dim H¹ ≥ 4` with even determinant; odd determinant with `tr M ≡ e₂(M)` and `dim H¹ ≥ 5`. "Theorem Fork for nonunit `σ` with `dim H¹ = 3`" is expected, not proved (only Lemmas 9 and 10 were re-derived without unimodularity). The generalisation "homological Pisot implies `cr ≠ 2` for every degree and norm" is a remark only, literature-facing and unreviewed. Recorded proof conditional on its imports (Barge 2015, Barge–Diamond 2008, Baker–Barge–Kwapisz 2006, standard facts not read in a source, the identification of the two coincidence ranks); single prover plus independent adversarial re-derivation; not machine-checked; vacuous on every computed PIP3 domain. Pinned class counts: `dim H¹ = 3` on 3,102 corpus specimens, 1,362 of them with even determinant (1,128 fail (F); 192 have (F) and an endpoint map with fewer than two periodic letters; 42 have (F) and two periodic letters on both sides); (F) and `dim H¹ = 3` on 1,350 = 1,116 odd + 234 even; of the 174 (F) specimens with `det M = 2` and two periodic letters on both sides, 42 are reached (30 with `χ_M ≡ t³`, 12 with `χ_M ≡ t(t²+t+1)` modulo 2) and 132 are not (72 with `d₂ = 1`, 60 with `d₂ = 2` and `dim H¹ = 5`). Locators: [rank-two note, Theorem H3](c4-rank-two-wall-2026-11-16.md); `kernel/tests/test_c4_geometric_f.mojo`.

- **2026-11-16 — Lemma S+ and Theorem P+: the parity mechanism for every determinant; at coincidence rank 2, `dim H¹ ≥ 3 + |d₂ − 1|`.** `d₂ = 3 − ord_t(χ_M mod 2)` is 3 for odd determinant, else 2 if `e₂` is odd, else 1 if `tr` is odd, else 0. Lemma S+ sharpens Lemma S of round 1: for `σ ∈ PIP3` with `cr = 2` and admissible `n`, the eventual range of Barge's ordered-pair substitution is two disjoint fixed edges exchanged by the swap, so 1 is an eigenvalue of `X − Y` (explicit swap-odd integer vector) and the removed factor is `(t − 1)`; with `nz χ_{X+Y} = χ_{Mⁿ} r` and `nz χ_{X−Y} = (t − 1) q₁`: `r q₁` divides `c_n`, and `h_n r̄ = (t + 1) q̄₁` over the field with two elements. Theorem P+ is the resulting dimension bound together with that divisor condition at every admissible `n`. Attribution (binding): it is the mechanism of the Barge–Bruin–Jones–Sadun theorem that a homological Pisot substitution of degree one has `cr ≠ 2`; Barge 2015 Thm 22 is the odd-norm case; Theorem P of round 1 is recovered from it at `d₂ = 3`, `n = 6`. For even determinant the dimension bound is subsumed by Theorem H3 (it reads `dim H¹ ≥ 4` for `d₂ ∈ {0, 2}` and nothing for `d₂ = 1`); beyond H3, P+ adds only the divisor condition, which is **not implemented**. Pinned: the dimension bound fails on 3,042 = 2,136 odd + 906 even corpus specimens; the hypotheses of "`cr ≠ 2`" without the divisor condition hold on 3,714 = 2,352 + 1,362. Not replayable, exploratory scratch only: the route's "24 further specimens excluded by the divisor condition" and its totals 930, 1,386 and 2,364. Imports: the Barge 2015 chain of Lemma S, the count of periodic words in the proof of its Thm 22 (which rests on the assertion there that the unordered-pair substitution is proper), Barge–Diamond 2008 Thm 4 with its proof, Lemma 7. Class counts, not certificates of a coincidence rank. Locators: [rank-two note, §7](c4-rank-two-wall-2026-11-16.md); `kernel/psc/c4_geometric_f.mojo`; `kernel/tests/test_c4_geometric_f.mojo`.

- **2026-11-16 — a strict system at coincidence rank 2 has one swap-symmetric primitive component (Theorem W), and its top-track map is not injective outside one unit case (Theorem NI).** For `σ ∈ PIP3` with `cr = 2` and a strict system: (W1, new) there is exactly one strict component; it is invariant under the swap and under `Φ×Φ`; its bigon alphabet is a strict system for `σ` itself with primitive child substitution, and it is the only sink of every strict system of every power; (W2, new) the top-track map `π₁` is injective over two-point fibres; (W3) `ρ(N₋) < β`, hence the first defect degree satisfies `r ≢ 1 (mod 3)`, with weights `(m,m,m)` or permutations of `(m+1,m+1,m)` (the sieve itself is on main: multidegree sieve note §8, degree-4 note Corollary B, Lean `no_strictComponent_deg4_of_hasBothSigns`; new are only the link to `cr = 2` and the nonunit side conditions); (W4) two partners of one tiling are `≈_s`-equivalent, which is the first half of the proof of Barge 2015 Lemma 13. Theorem NI, for a swap-symmetric primitive component with first defect degree `r` (any determinant, any `dim H¹`): `π₁` injective on periodic points forces `|det M| = 1`, `3 | r` and weights `(r/3, r/3, r/3)`; otherwise the inflation-fixed tilings with at least two fixed partners, counted with the number of their extra partners, number at least `c ρⁿ` for infinitely many `n`, `ρ` the largest root modulus of `χ_N/χ_{M^P}`. Prior art (binding): "`N₋` is the signed matrix, the Parikh quotient is deck-even, the signed lowest-defect intertwiner" are Proposition 1 and §3 of the orientation-spectrum note; Lean covers the defect intertwiner through `r ≤ 5`, prose beyond. New against Theorem Fork: every determinant and every `dim H¹`, with a weaker conclusion (the second partner may be eventually coincident with the first); so the unique-partner premise of the retired derived-substitution rigidity fails beyond the Fork class as well, outside that one unit case. Theorem TT, used for NI and not on main: `π₁` injective on inflation-periodic points forces `χ_N/χ_{M^P}` to be a product of powers of `x` and cyclotomic polynomials; the converse is false (`1/012/101`, C-D2; scratch). Lemma U: a monomial in the roots of an irreducible Pisot cubic is a root of unity only with equal exponents and `|det M| = 1` (a special case of a theorem of Mignotte, 1984, per secondary sources; proof kept). Not proved, do not write: "at `cr = 2`, `σ` is unit and `3 | r`"; "no strict system for nonunit `σ` at `cr = 2`". Conditional on imports; not machine-checked; vacuous on every computed PIP3 domain; all control spectra are scratch. Locators: [rank-two note, §§3–6](c4-rank-two-wall-2026-11-16.md); [orientation-spectrum note](c4-orientation-spectrum.md).

- **2026-11-16 — exact count of inflation-fixed tilings (Theorem FP), pinned; standard, do not claim it.** For a primitive substitution on at least two letters, `|Fix(Φⁿ)| = tr(Aⁿ) − #Fix(gⁿ) − #Fix(g_Rⁿ) + #{ba legal : g_Rⁿ(b) = b, gⁿ(a) = a}`: interior occurrences of a letter in its own `n`-th image, plus the seams of periodic two-letter words. It is the Anderson–Putnam zeta function restated (primary text not read). `kernel/psc/fixed_point_count.mojo` evaluates the formula and, independently, counts reproduced pointed legal windows on literal words; they agree part by part for `n = 1, …, 6` on 11 substitutions (five corpus specimens, the reducible controls `011/120/2012`, `1/012/101`, `10/102/211`, the periodic `1/012/1`, Fibonacci and Thue–Morse). Worth not recomputing: Fibonacci gives `L_n − 1`, Thue–Morse `2ⁿ − 1 + (−1)ⁿ`, the periodic control `2ⁿ − 1`. Counts of periodic points only: no coincidence rank or spectrum is certified. Locators: [rank-two note, §2](c4-rank-two-wall-2026-11-16.md); `kernel/tests/test_fixed_point_count.mojo`.

- **2026-11-16 — Obstruction T: the first-defect class of a rank-two strict system does not descend to Barge's quotient; the transport route is retired as posed.** For a hypothetical strict system at `cr = 2` whose first-defect eigenvalue `λ` is not a root of unity (always for nonunit `σ` or `3 ∤ r`), the `λ`-part of `H¹` of the pair space meets the pull-back of `H¹(Ω)` under the top-track map only in 0. So the defect class does not descend to Barge's quotient `Ω/≈_s`, and Lemma S (every nonzero eigenvalue of the signed overlap matrix `X − Y` is a root of unity) cannot be applied to it. Do not re-run "carry the first-defect eigenvalue of `N₋` into `X − Y` and contradict Lemma S": its first premise fails, and in the remaining case (unit, weights `(m,m,m)`) `λ = ±1` contradicts nothing. This is an obstruction to the method. It is not a consistency statement: "Lemma S and the defect intertwiner are jointly consistent in every case" is not proved (no model exists in PIP3) and must not be written. The warning example for soft arguments stands: `a → aaaab, b → abbbb` is an almost everywhere one-to-one extension that adds the expanding eigenvalue 3. The growth comparison of the same route (multi-partner growth against a boundary growth rate) is heuristic and unverified, and is not recorded as a statement. Locators: [rank-two note, §9](c4-rank-two-wall-2026-11-16.md); [closure-attempt note §15.4](c4-closure-attempt-2026-11-16.md).

- **2026-11-16 — the non-trivial part of the FC3 class is infinite (two explicit families, nonunit members of every odd determinant); it adds no C4 case.** `σ_{r,s,n} = (1^r, 0 1^n 2, 0 1^s)` has `χ = t³ − n t² − (r+s) t − r`, `det M = r`; it is in PIP3 for `n ≥ r + s + 1` and satisfies the geometric property (F) for every `n ≥ 3r + s`; `τ_n = (1, 2 1^n 0, 01)` is in PIP3 for `n ≥ 1` and satisfies (F) for `n ≥ 5`. (F) is proved uniformly: the state map acts on two coordinates by the integer recursion of the contracting quadratic factor with a bounded rounding term; a trapping lemma (`ε + 2r/β ≤ 1`, equivalent to `n ≥ 3r + s`) confines every cycle to four classes, and a class table sends each to a vertex state in at most 4 steps (6 for `τ_n`). `dim H¹ = 4` for `s ≥ 1` and 5 for `s = 0` and for `τ_n`. For `r` odd and `s ≥ 1` (clause (b)), `r` odd, `s = 0`, `n` even (clause (c) only) and `τ_n` with `n` odd (clause (c) only) the members lie in the non-trivial part of the FC3 class, and no two members are related by powers, relabelling or reversal (the Perron column and the periodic letters of the endpoint maps separate them); this discharges the round-1 non-claim. **Every member also has explicit left and right strong-coincidence witnesses at levels at most 3, so "no strict system" is elementary on them (Theorem A(4)) and none needs clause (b) or (c): these are not new C4 cases.** Not new either: the root location (non-increasing coefficients, Frougny–Solomyak as quoted by Akiyama) and the method (the dominant-coefficient argument of the shift-radix and β-numeration finiteness theory, applied to a three-branch variant). The letter order `(1^r, 1^n 0 2, 1^s 0)` of the same matrix is a β-substitution in disguise with `dim H¹ = 3`: compare letter orders, not matrices. Pinned on a grid (`r ≤ 5`, `s ≤ 2`, `n ≤ 3r + s + 6`, 240 members, 220 in PIP3; `τ_n`, `n ≤ 16`): (F) at 210 members, at all 105 with `n ≥ 3r + s` and at every `τ_n`; (F) fails at exactly ten members, all with `n ≤ r + s − 1` (periods 5; 14; 8; 5; 5; 8,8,8,8; 5; 23; 8,8,8,14; 8 for `(r,s,n) = (2,1,1), (3,0,2), (3,1,2), (3,2,2), (3,2,3), (4,1,3), (4,2,3), (5,0,4), (5,1,4), (5,2,4)`), so the shape alone does not give (F). Expected, not proved: (F) for every `n ≥ r + s`; before pursuing it, read Frougny–Solomyak 1992 and the proofs behind the shift-radix survey (the threshold is their non-increasing-digit condition). Recorded proofs, single prover plus two adversarial re-derivations, not machine-checked. Locators: [family note](c4-fc3-family-2026-11-16.md); [family stop/go note](c4-fc3-family-stop-go-2026-11-16.md); `kernel/psc/c4_fc3_family.mojo`; `kernel/tests/test_c4_fc3_family.mojo`.

- **2026-11-16 — withdrawn: "`σ_{1,s,n}` and `τ_n` are not products of Brun or Jacobi–Perron substitutions".** The round-2 family route recorded this as proved; it is false. With the Jacobi–Perron substitutions as printed by Berthé–Bourdon–Jolivet–Siegel (`σ_{a,b}: 0 → 2, 1 → 0 2^a, 2 → 1 2^b` for all `a, b ≥ 0`), `τ_n = σ_{0,0} σ_{n,0} σ_{0,1} σ_{0,0} σ_{0,1}`, and the reversal of `σ_{1,0,n}`, relabelled cyclically, is `σ_{n,0} σ_{0,1}`. The invariant used ("the last-letter map sends two letters to a fixed letter") holds only for `b ≥ 1`, and the supporting exhaustive search had enumerated admissible factors only. What survives: `σ_{1,s,n}` with `s ≥ 1` is not a Brun or Jacobi–Perron product in either orientation (non-bijective first-letter maps); `σ_{1,0,n}`, `τ_n` and their reversals are not Brun products (printed letter orderings) and not Jacobi–Perron-admissible products, so the pure-discrete-spectrum theorem for admissible products does not cover them. The Jacobi–Perron literature beyond admissible products was not examined. Also corrected in the same comparison: β-substitutions have an eventually constant first-letter map with a fixed letter, not a constant one; the word `0 1^n 2` is a cyclic rotation of the Mercat–Akiyama word `1^n 2 0` (the substitutions are not conjugate, and `dim H¹` is 5 against 3). Tooling lesson: a search restricted by a hypothesis must be reported with that hypothesis. Locators: [family stop/go note](c4-fc3-family-stop-go-2026-11-16.md); [family note §11](c4-fc3-family-2026-11-16.md).

- **2026-11-16 — Theorem FC1 is machine-checked in Lean, with property (F) as the one remaining hypothesis; (F) itself is not machine-checked for any three-letter substitution.** Machine-checked (Lean 4, toolchain `v4.34.0-rc2`; axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`): (i) the abstract form, modules `PscVerif.GeometricFInclusion` and `PscVerif.GeometricFStrict`: under `LengthData`, `β > 1`, `ContractionData` and `PropertyF`, the inclusion `M^{mN}(V_X ∪ V_X′) ⊆ V_X ∩ V_X′`, the gap bound, the strict system built from the common cuts, and the equivalence "a strict system exists iff some pair of distinct `g`-periodic letters has no left strong-coincidence witness iff not every pair has one" (`fc1_inclusion_gap_of_contraction`, `fc1_strictSystem`, `fc1_equivalence_of_contraction`); the "Powers" direction, prose only until now (`strictSystem_of_power`); (ii) the binding to the standing class, module `PscVerif.GeometricFPisot`: for a primitive substitution on a finite alphabet with at least two letters, irreducible `χ_M`, a positive left eigen-row and the Pisot hypothesis in root form, the abstract hypotheses hold (`fc1_hypotheses_of_primitive_pisot`; irreducibility enters only through separability of `χ_M`, the Pisot property only through the contraction), hence `fc1_equivalence_of_primitive_pisot` and, on three letters, `fc1_pip3`; (iii) non-vacuity and controls: Thue–Morse satisfies the abstract hypotheses and has its strict system; Fibonacci (two letters) satisfies every hypothesis including (F), and the three statements are false there; Tribonacci satisfies everything except that (F) is not shown; the reducible strict controls `10/102/211`, `01/201/112`, `011/120/2012`, `010/1222/2012`, `1/012/101` admit no contraction data for any length data with `β > 1`. **Not machine-checked:** (F) for any substitution on three letters, so no PIP3 specimen meets all hypotheses of `fc1_pip3` in Lean; Lemmas 1(1), 1(3), 4, 5 of the (F)-class note; Theorems FC2, P, FC3, N, Fork; anything of round 2 outside FC1. The theorem is an equivalence and excludes no strict system. Recording gate still open: the isolated checker `tools/check_lean.sh` was not run on these modules (incremental builds and module-restricted axiom audits only), so formal audit credit needs the CI receipt; no ledger claim is registered. Locators: `proof/PscVerif/PscVerif/GeometricFInclusion.lean`; `proof/PscVerif/PscVerif/GeometricFStrict.lean`; `proof/PscVerif/PscVerif/GeometricFPisot.lean`; [closure-attempt note §15.2](c4-closure-attempt-2026-11-16.md).

- **2026-11-16 — round-1 statements of the C4 closure attempt superseded by round 2 (dated corrections stand beneath the earlier text).** (a) Literature gate of the (F)-class note, table of non-transferring hypotheses: "every class of `H¹(Ω; ℤ)` is an eigenfunction class: none in print" is wrong; it is in print for every determinant once the ranks agree (Barge–Kellendonk–Schmieding 2012, arXiv:1204.1432, Cor. 3 and Thm 5; not found in round 1). (b) (F)-class note §7: "that is where this argument uses unimodularity and `l = 0`" and "Whether the conclusion of Theorem Fork holds without them is not known here": Lemma 9 and Lemma 10 hold without unimodularity when `dim H¹ = 3`, which gives Theorem H3; the extension of Theorem Fork itself to nonunit `σ` is expected, not proved. (c) (F)-class note, non-claims: "Even determinant. Nothing beyond FC1 and FC2" is superseded by H3, FC3+, N+ and P+; "Infinitude of the non-trivial part of the FC3 class. Not shown" is discharged by the family note; "none is machine-checked" is superseded for Theorem FC1. (d) Closure-attempt note: "nothing is known for `dim H¹ ≥ 4` or for nonunit `σ`" (§3, §10 item 5) is superseded by Theorem NI and Theorem H3; "354 of the 510" (§10 item 1) becomes 312; "Nothing is machine-checked" (§4) holds except for FC1. (e) The route's own wordings not adopted: "Lean covers the defect intertwiner only for `r = 2, 3, 4`" (main has degree 5 as well); "H3 at `|det| = 1` is Barge 2015 Cor. 25" (it is Thm 22, for every odd determinant); "jointly consistent in every case" (not proved). Locators: [closure-attempt note §15.5](c4-closure-attempt-2026-11-16.md); [(F)-class note, round-2 update](c4-geometric-f-class-2026-11-16.md); [(F)-class literature gate, round-2 update](c4-geometric-f-class-literature-2026-11-16.md).

- **2026-11-16 — tooling pitfalls from round 2 of the C4 closure attempt.** (i) `psc.c4_geometric_f.decide_geometric_f` and `fc3_profile` are certified for images of at most six letters and refuse longer ones: of the proved (F) regime of the families they accept only `σ_{1,0,3}`, `σ_{1,0,4}`, `σ_{1,1,4}` and no `τ_n` with `n ≥ 5`. The family regression asserts that refusal and decides longer images with `psc.c4_fc3_family.decide_f_screened`, which is not the canonical verdict; it is guarded by agreement with the canonical decision on all 4,554 corpus specimens, by pinned radii with an empty doubled box, and by the literal box graph of Corollary LC5 on six members. (ii) The Routh screen `psc.pisot_screen.screen` returns a refusal, neither verdict, at the PIP3 family members with `n = 2r − s − 3` (nine on the grid; `σ_{4,0,5}` and `σ_{5,0,7}` lie in the range where PIP3 is proved): there the verdict is that of `psc.pisot` alone, and a refusal must not be read as a rejection. (iii) A counter-calibration must be computed, not expected: radii `(0,0,0)` were expected to miss the 5-cycle of `σ_{2,1,1}`, but that box contains a cycle state; the pinned control is `σ_{3,0,2}` (the expectation was replaced, not tuned). (iv) The divisor condition of Theorem P+ and the refinement at admissible `n < 6` are not implemented: the route's figures 930, 1,386, 2,364 and "24 further specimens" are not replayable, and the replayable dimension-bound figure for even determinant is 906. (v) `psc.fixed_point_count` accepts primitive substitutions on 2 to 8 letters; its literal count is a count of reproduced pointed windows in which each vertex-type tiling is seen once from each of its two tiles, and it raises if the two seam lists disagree. (vi) The coincidence automaton is exact at every image length but slow on some long-image members (about 26 s for the six pair questions of `σ_{5,2,17}`). (vii) State the image-length domain next to any (F) verdict: the landing task asked for the canonical decision on the proved regime, where the module refuses all but three grid members. (viii) Lean: an incremental `lake build` of one module and a module-restricted `#print axioms` audit are not the isolated checker; the README of `proof/PscVerif` asks for `tools/check_lean.sh`. Locators: `kernel/tests/test_c4_fc3_family.mojo`; `kernel/tests/test_c4_geometric_f.mojo`; `kernel/tests/test_fixed_point_count.mojo`; [family note §6, §12](c4-fc3-family-2026-11-16.md).

- **2026-11-16 — FC2 rank transfer across orientations.** BBK's terminal-endpoint exclusion requires reflection of whole strands or a common interior translation for the right lower bound. The return-lattice frequency proof applies at every determinant. The BBK and Barge 2015 ranks agree via Barge–Kellendonk §2.2 and the Haar-null images of the extra strand orbits. The integer Lean transfer reuses FC1 vertex absorption and takes the geometric rank realizations as hypotheses. Locator: [FC2 rank-transfer review](c4-fc2-rank-transfer-review-2026-11-16.md).

- **2026-11-16 — corrections to the round-2 entries of 2026-11-16 above (report-only audit of the notes, applied after the merge).** The round-2 entries above were merged with PR #590 (snapshot `abcc8f4a`) before two independent report-only audits of the round-2 notes were applied. The audits reported no error in a proof; they reported the following, and the entries above stand as written except where corrected here. (a) *Family entry, false sentence:* "Every member also has explicit left and right strong-coincidence witnesses at levels at most 3" is false for the members with `s = 0`. Proposition FM5 gives explicit left witnesses at levels at most 3 for `σ_{r,s,n}`, `n ≥ r + 1`, and for `τ_n`, `n ≥ 2`, and explicit right witnesses at levels at most 3 only for `s ≥ 1`, `n ≥ s`. Right witnesses for `s = 0` and for `τ_n` are not part of the written proposition; pinned on the grid, the least right levels are `(3,4,4)` for `s = 0`, `n ≥ r + 1` (so the pairs `{0,2}` and `{1,2}` have no right witness at level 3 or below) and `(2,1,2)` for `τ_n`, `2 ≤ n ≤ 16`. The conclusion drawn there ("no strict system" by Theorem A(4); not new C4 cases) needs left witnesses only and stands. (b) *Family entry, verification status:* "no two members are related by powers, relabelling or reversal", under the label "single prover plus two adversarial re-derivations": this separation is Proposition FM6, which was written at landing and seen by neither verifier; the verifiers confirmed the weaker statement for fixed `(r, s)`, and the grid pins relabelling and reversal only (512 normal forms), nothing on powers. (c) *Family entry, scope:* the letter order `(1^r, 1^n 0 2, 1^s 0)` is a β-substitution in disguise only when `n (r+s) r` is the expansion of 1, i.e. for `n ≥ r + s` (Parry's condition, quoted from memory in the family gate); its first-letter map is eventually constant (`(1,1,0)` for `s = 0`; the family gate said "constant" and is corrected). (d) *Withdrawn Brun/Jacobi–Perron entry:* in "What survives", "`σ_{1,s,n}` with `s ≥ 1` is not a Brun or Jacobi–Perron product in either orientation" holds for Brun substitutions with the printed letter orderings only (reordered Brun substitutions are not excluded); for Jacobi–Perron substitutions it holds as stated, up to relabelling. The family gate had not confronted the entry of 2026-10-11 of this ledger on unordered Brun composites (mirror Barge class); it now cites it and claims nothing from it, because the letter convention of that programme was not checked. (e) *Lean entry, item (i):* the hypotheses were listed jointly; the statements are: `fc1_inclusion_gap_of_contraction` under `LengthData`, `ContractionData`, `PropertyF` (no `β > 1`); `fc1_strictSystem` under `LengthData`, `β > 1`, finiteness of the relative states and "every relative state reaches a vertex state" (both supplied by `ContractionData` and `PropertyF`: `relStates_finite_of_contraction`, `reaches_vertex_of_propertyF`), for two fixed words whose first letters have no left witness; `fc1_equivalence_of_contraction` under `LengthData`, `β > 1`, `ContractionData`, `PropertyF`; `strictSystem_of_power` for nonerasing `σ` and `m ≥ 1`. The slip was on the conservative side. (f) *Tooling entry, item (i):* "guarded by agreement with the canonical decision on all 4,554 corpus specimens" overstates the pin: on all 4,554 specimens the screened walk inside the canonical box reproduces the canonical interior cycles; `decide_f_screened` end to end agrees with the canonical decision on every 23rd specimen (198). (g) *Theorem W / NI entry:* "the converse is false (`1/012/101`, C-D2; scratch)" for Theorem TT: both controls are reducible, so only the converse of the general form (any primitive `σ`) is shown false; on PIP3 the converse is untested on strict systems, none being known. (h) *Obstruction T entry:* "`λ = ±1` contradicts nothing" should read: `±1` is a root of unity, so Lemma S is not contradicted; whether the transport exists in that case, or is compatible with items 4 and 5 of Lemma S+, was not examined. (i) *Unreported refusal (family note §12, exploratory):* the scratch figure "below `n = r + s`, `r ≤ 7`, `s ≤ 4`: (F) held at 53 PIP3 members and failed at 30" dropped a refusal: there are 84 such members, and the landing script refused `σ_{7,0,6}` (second root modulus about 0.9915); the regression now pins one interior cycle of period 32 there (`test_long_image_members_failing_f`), so the split is 53 and 31. Lesson: report refusals next to counts. (j) *Screened decision beyond six letters (tooling-pitfalls entry, item on the family regression):* no long-image grid member fails (F), so the regression was blind in the negative direction there; five negative controls off the grid, with images of 7 to 25 letters, now pin interior cycles found by the screened walk (`σ_{6,1,5}`, `σ_{7,0,6}`, `1/0 1^k 2/001` for `k = 5, 12, 23`). The notes carry the same corrections with the merged wordings kept on record. Locators: [rank-two note §12](c4-rank-two-wall-2026-11-16.md); [family note §12](c4-fc3-family-2026-11-16.md); [family stop/go note](c4-fc3-family-stop-go-2026-11-16.md); [rank-two stop/go note](c4-rank-two-wall-stop-go-2026-11-16.md); [closure-attempt note §15.8](c4-closure-attempt-2026-11-16.md); `kernel/tests/test_c4_fc3_family.mojo`; `proof/PscVerif/PscVerif/GeometricFInclusion.lean`; `proof/PscVerif/PscVerif/GeometricFStrict.lean`.

(1) The divisor condition of Theorem P+, implemented and pinned in Mojo (next entry). (2) Property (F) for Tribonacci, machine-checked in Lean, giving the first complete instance of `fc1_pip3` on three letters. - **2026-11-16 — the divisor condition of Theorem P+ is implemented (`psc.rank_two_parity`); beyond the earlier hypotheses it excludes coincidence rank 2, conditionally, on 36 corpus specimens, and on none of the open (F) classes.** Exact and fail closed: `f = g_K` on the eventual range as a graph map (reused from `psc.h1_census`, refused when `ER` is disconnected or `f` is not a permutation), `c_n` from the cycle type of `fⁿ` (Proposition C of the module docstring, cross-checked against Newton's identities on Lefschetz traces and against trial division), `h_n` from `(M mod 2)ⁿ`, and an exhaustive search over cyclotomic sub-multisets; `n = 1, …, 42` decides every admissible `n` (Lemma N of the module: the data depend on `n mod 42`). Pinned on the 4,554 corpus specimens: the condition fails on 2,364 odd and 930 even; beyond the earlier hypotheses (odd: `dim H¹ ≤ 4` or `tr ≢ e₂`; even: `dim H¹ = 3`) on 12 odd (`d₂ = 3`, `dim H¹ = 5`) and 24 even (`d₂ = 2`, `dim H¹ = 4`) specimens, all first failing at `n = 2`, none with (F), all with two periodic letters on both sides; on the (F) class it fails on 1,470 specimens, all inside the earlier hypotheses; on the open (F) classes (132 even, 180 odd) on none, so those classes are unchanged. From the pinned cell table: no recorded necessary condition of `cr = 2` fails on 540 even and 264 odd corpus specimens. Class counts under a recorded conditional theorem, not certificates of a coincidence rank; the corpus has no strict system. Locators: `kernel/tests/test_rank_two_parity.mojo`; `kernel/psc/rank_two_parity.mojo`; [round-3 note §2](c4-round3-2026-11-16.md).
